\displaystyle 4|\alpha_{j+4}-\alpha_{j+3}|^{2}-6|\alpha_{j+4}-\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}+4|\alpha_{j+4}-\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}-|\alpha_{j+4}-\alpha_{j}|^{2}

\displaystyle+24|\alpha_{j+3}-\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}-16|\alpha_{j+3}-\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}+4|\alpha_{j+3}-\alpha_{j}|^{2}

\displaystyle+24|\alpha_{j+2}-\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}-6|\alpha_{j+2}-\alpha_{j}|^{2}+4|\alpha_{j+1}-\alpha_{j}|^{2}

which implying

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{16}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j+4}-4\alpha_{j+3}+6\alpha_{j+2}-4\alpha_{j+1}+\alpha_{j}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{bdy}-\frac{7}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+\frac{7}{4}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j+1}-\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j+2}-\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{16}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j+3}-\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}

with boundary

\displaystyle\mathrm{bdy}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{j=0}^{3}|\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{2}|\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{1}|\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}|\alpha_{0}+1|^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{2}|\alpha_{j+1}-\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}-\sum_{j=0}^{1}|\alpha_{j+1}-\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}-\frac{3}{8}|\alpha_{1}+1|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{j=0}^{1}|\alpha_{j+2}-\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}|\alpha_{2}+1|^{2}-\frac{1}{16}|\alpha_{3}+1|^{2}.

∎

Theorem 4.68
.

Assume
\alpha\in\ell^{6}
and
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{3}
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}(1-\cos\theta)^{4}\log w(\theta)\frac{d\theta}{2\pi}>-\infty\,\,\Longleftrightarrow\,\,(S-1)^{4}\alpha\in\ell^{2}.

(4.180)

Remark 4.69
.

This is again a one-case result of the original Simon conjecture under certain conditions.

Proof.

By Lemma 4.3 and applying (4.154), (4.155) and (4.161) to the sum rule (4.176), we have that if
\alpha\in\ell^{6}
and
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{3}
, then
\mathrm{CP}<+\infty
and the series in
\mathrm{EP}
are convergent except the following one

-\frac{1}{16}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j+3}-4\alpha_{j+2}+6\alpha_{j+1}-4\alpha_{j}+\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}.

(4.181)

So
Z_{4,4}(\mu)>-\infty
if and only if
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j+3}-4\alpha_{j+2}+6\alpha_{j+1}-4\alpha_{j}+\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}<+\infty
(i.e.,
(S-1)^{4}\alpha\in\ell^{2}
) as
\alpha\in\ell^{6}
and
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{3}
.
∎

Theorem 4.70
.

Assume
\alpha\in\ell^{4}
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}(1-\cos\theta)^{4}\log w(\theta)\frac{d\theta}{2\pi}>-\infty\,\,\Longleftrightarrow\,\,(S-1)^{4}\alpha\in\ell^{2}.

(4.182)

Remark 4.71
.

This is again one special case of a result in
[
7
]
due to Golinskii and Zlatoš.

Proof.

As
\alpha\in\ell^{4}
, by the sum rule (4.176) and Hölder inequality, we have that the series in it are convergent except the only one (4.180). Thus
Z_{4,4}(\mu)>-\infty
if and only if
(S-1)^{4}\alpha\in\ell^{2}
as
\alpha\in\ell^{4}
.
∎

Theorem 4.72
.

Assume
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{2}
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}(1-\cos\theta)^{4}\log w(\theta)\frac{d\theta}{2\pi}>-\infty\,\,\Longleftrightarrow\,\,\alpha\in\ell^{10}.

(4.183)

Remark 4.73
.

This is one special case of the result in
[
9
]
due to Lukic.

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{4}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j}|^{6}-\frac{1}{8}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(|\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}(|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j}|^{2})+|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\big)(|\alpha_{j+3}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\big)\Big[|\alpha_{j+2}|^{4}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{4}+|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}

\displaystyle+2|\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}+2|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\Big]+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\Big[|\alpha_{j}|^{4}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\big(|\alpha_{j+1}|^{4}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{4}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(|\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2})|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\Big[|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{4}+|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{4}+|\alpha_{j}|^{4}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+1}|^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{7}{12}-\frac{7}{16}|\alpha_{0}|^{2}-\frac{3}{8}|\alpha_{0}|^{4}+\frac{1}{16}|\alpha_{1}|^{2}+\frac{1}{16}|\alpha_{1}|^{4}-\frac{5}{16}|\alpha_{1}|^{2}|\alpha_{0}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{16}|\alpha_{2}|^{2}|\alpha_{1}|^{2}+\frac{1}{16}|\alpha_{2}|^{2}|\alpha_{0}|^{2}+\frac{1}{16}\sum_{j=0}^{1}|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{16}\sum_{j=0}^{1}|\alpha_{j}|^{4}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{1}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{4}+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{j=0}^{1}|\alpha_{j}|^{6}

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{2|\alpha_{j}|^{6}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{6}}{3}-|\alpha_{j}|^{4}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{|\alpha_{j}|^{6}+2|\alpha_{j-1}|^{6}}{3}-|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{4}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{2|\alpha_{j+1}|^{6}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{6}}{3}-|\alpha_{j+1}|^{4}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{|\alpha_{j+1}|^{6}+2|\alpha_{j-1}|^{6}}{3}-|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{4}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{|\alpha_{j+2}|^{6}+|\alpha_{j+1}|^{6}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{6}}{3}-|\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{|\alpha_{j+2}|^{6}+|\alpha_{j}|^{6}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{6}}{3}-|\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{|\alpha_{j+1}|^{6}+|\alpha_{j}|^{6}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{6}}{3}-|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\big)

(4.184)

and

\displaystyle-\frac{17}{16}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j}|^{8}+\frac{1}{8}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\big(|\alpha_{j+3}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\Big[|\alpha_{j+1}|^{6}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{6}+|\alpha_{j}|^{4}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\Big[|\alpha_{j+2}|^{4}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{4}+|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}

\displaystyle+2|\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}+2|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\Big]+\frac{3}{4}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j}|^{4}\Big[|\alpha_{j+1}|^{4}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{4}\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{13}{32}-\frac{1}{16}|\alpha_{0}|^{2}-\frac{3}{32}|\alpha_{0}|^{4}-\frac{1}{16}|\alpha_{0}|^{6}-\frac{1}{16}|\alpha_{1}|^{2}|\alpha_{0}|^{2}-\frac{1}{16}|\alpha_{1}|^{4}|\alpha_{0}|^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}|\alpha_{1}|^{2}|\alpha_{0}|^{4}-\frac{1}{16}|\alpha_{2}|^{2}|\alpha_{1}|^{2}|\alpha_{0}|^{2}-\frac{1}{32}\sum_{j=0}^{1}|\alpha_{j}|^{8}-\frac{3}{16}|\alpha_{0}|^{8}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{|\alpha_{j+2}|^{8}+|\alpha_{j+1}|^{8}+|\alpha_{j}|^{8}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{8}}{4}-|\alpha_{j+2}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{3|\alpha_{j}|^{8}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{8}}{4}-|\alpha_{j}|^{6}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{|\alpha_{j}|^{8}+3|\alpha_{j-1}|^{8}}{4}-|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{6}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{|\alpha_{j+1}|^{8}+2|\alpha_{j}|^{8}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{8}}{4}-|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{4}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{2|\alpha_{j+1}|^{8}+|\alpha_{j}|^{8}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{8}}{4}-|\alpha_{j+1}|^{4}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{|\alpha_{j+1}|^{8}+|\alpha_{j}|^{8}+2|\alpha_{j-1}|^{8}}{4}-|\alpha_{j+1}|^{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{4}\big)

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{16}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(\frac{|\alpha_{j}|^{8}+|\alpha_{j-1}|^{8}}{2}-|\alpha_{j}|^{4}|\alpha_{j-1}|^{4}\big),

(4.185)

then if
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{2}
, by (4.124), (4.161) and the sum rule (4.176), we have the series in
\mathrm{CP}
and
\mathrm{EP}
are finite except the following one

\displaystyle\frac{35}{8}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\Big(\log(1-|\alpha_{j}|^{2})+|\alpha_{j}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{4}+\frac{1}{3}|\alpha_{j}|^{6}+\frac{1}{4}|\alpha_{j}|^{8}\Big).

Thus, by Lemma 4.3, we get that

Z_{4,4}(\mu)>-\infty
is equivalent to
\alpha\in\ell^{10}
as
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{2}
.
∎

As Theorems 4.58 and 4.66, with respect to one-directional implication from
\alpha
to
Z_{4,4}(\mu)
, we have

Theorem 4.74
.

Assume
\alpha\in\ell^{6}
and
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{2}
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}(1-\cos\theta)^{4}\log w(\theta)\frac{d\theta}{2\pi}>-\infty.

(4.186)

Proof.

For a direct proof, similar to Theorem 4.68, it mainly follows from (4.154) and (4.155). For an indirect proof, note that
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{2}
implies
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{3}
and
(S-1)^{4}\alpha\in\ell^{2}
, it is a consequence of Theorem 4.68. More immediately, it is a consequence of Theorem 4.72 since
\alpha\in\ell^{6}
implies
\alpha\in\ell^{10}
.
∎

5.
Sum rules and higher order Szegő theorems: General cases

In the last section, a few of sum rules and higher order Szegő theorems have been established in some concrete cases. One can find that there exist some common characteristics in these known results (for instance, see Theorems 4.49, 4.52, 4.60 and 4.68). In fact, these common characteristics also appear in higher order Szegő theorems of all other cases. In this section, we will develop general sum rules and higher order Szegő theorems. More importantly, an expression of
w_{m}
with a single infinite index is given for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

At first, we generalize (4.87), (4.105) and their analogues in higher order Szegő theorems in forth order case. These generalized results can be used in the calculations for some analogous higher order Szegő theorem of all orders as the ones in the last section.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\alpha=\{\alpha_{j}\}_{0}^{\infty}\in\ell^{2}
and
S
be the left shift operator on sequences satisfying
(S\alpha)_{j}=\alpha_{j+1}
, then

\displaystyle\|(S-1)^{k}\alpha\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}-1}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}\|(S^{k-l}-S^{k-l^{\prime}})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

(5.1)

and

\displaystyle\|(S+1)^{k}\alpha\|_{2}^{2}=2^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{k}C_{k}^{l}\|S^{k-l}\alpha\|^{2}-\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}\|(S^{k-l}-S^{k-l^{\prime}})\alpha\|_{2}^{2},

(5.2)

where
C_{k}^{l}
is the binomial coefficient.
More generally,

\displaystyle\|P_{k}(S)\alpha\|_{2}^{2}=P_{k}(1)\sum_{l=0}^{k}a_{l}\|S^{l}\alpha\|^{2}-\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}a_{l}a_{l^{\prime}}\|(S^{l}-S^{l^{\prime}})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

(5.3)

where
P_{k}(x)=a_{0}+a_{1}x+\cdots+a_{k}x^{k}
with
a_{l}\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq l\leq k
.

Proof.

It is enough to prove (5.1). Similar is to (5.2) and (5.3). Note that

\displaystyle\big((S-1)^{k}\alpha\big)_{j}=\Big[\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{k}(-1)^{l}C_{k}^{l}S^{k-l}\Big)\alpha\Big]_{j}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}(-1)^{l}C_{k}^{l}\big(S^{k-l}\alpha\big)_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}(-1)^{l}C_{k}^{l}\alpha_{j+k-l},

(5.4)

then

\displaystyle\big|\big((S-1)^{k}\alpha\big)_{j}\big|^{2}=\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k}(-1)^{l}C_{k}^{l}\alpha_{j+k-l}\right)\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k}(-1)^{l}C_{k}^{l}\overline{\alpha}_{j+k-l}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{l^{\prime}=0}^{k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}\alpha_{j+k-l}\overline{\alpha}_{j+k-l^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}\big(C_{k}^{l}\big)^{2}|\alpha_{j+k-l}|^{2}+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}\big(\alpha_{j+k-l}\overline{\alpha}_{j+k-l^{\prime}}+\overline{\alpha}_{j+k-l}\alpha_{j+k-l^{\prime}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}\big(C_{k}^{l}\big)^{2}|\alpha_{j+k-l}|^{2}+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}\big(|\alpha_{j+k-l}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j+k-l^{\prime}}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}-1}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}|\alpha_{j+k-l}-\alpha_{j+k-l^{\prime}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}\big(C_{k}^{l}\big)^{2}|\alpha_{j+k-l}|^{2}+\sum_{0\leq l\neq l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}|\alpha_{j+k-l}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}-1}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}|\alpha_{j+k-l}-\alpha_{j+k-l^{\prime}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{l^{\prime}=0}^{k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}|\alpha_{j+k-l}|^{2}+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}-1}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}|\alpha_{j+k-l}-\alpha_{j+k-l^{\prime}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}(-1)^{l}C_{k}^{l}|\alpha_{j+k-l}|^{2}\sum_{l^{\prime}=0}^{k}(-1)^{l^{\prime}}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}-1}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}|\alpha_{j+k-l}-\alpha_{j+k-l^{\prime}}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}-1}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}|\alpha_{j+k-l}-\alpha_{j+k-l^{\prime}}|^{2}.

(5.5)

Thus (5.1) immediately follows from (5.5).
∎

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
\alpha=\{\alpha_{j}\}_{0}^{\infty}\in\ell^{2}
and
S
be the left shift operator, then

\displaystyle\|(S-1)^{m+k}(S+1)^{k}\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq p<p^{\prime}\leq m}\,\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{p+p^{\prime}+l+l^{\prime}}C_{m}^{p}C_{m}^{p^{\prime}}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}\|\big(S^{m-p}-S^{m-p^{\prime}}\big)\big(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})}\big)\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

(5.6)

and

\displaystyle\|(S+1)^{m+k}(S-1)^{k}\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{m}\sum_{p=0}^{m}\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}-1}C_{m}^{p}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}\|S^{m-p}(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq p<p^{\prime}\leq m}\,\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}}C_{m}^{p}C_{m}^{p^{\prime}}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}\|\big(S^{m-p}-S^{m-p^{\prime}}\big)\big(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})}\big)\alpha\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.7)

Proof.

Since

\displaystyle(S-1)^{k}(S+1)^{k}=(S^{2}-1)^{2}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}(-1)^{l}C_{k}^{l}S^{2(k-l)},

(5.8)

by a similar argument to (5.1), we have

\displaystyle\|(S^{2}-1)^{k}\beta\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}(-1)^{l+l^{\prime}-1}C_{k}^{l}C_{k}^{l^{\prime}}\|(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\beta\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.9)

Let
\beta=(S-1)^{m}\alpha
and
\gamma=(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\alpha
, then

(S^{2}-1)^{k}\beta=(S-1)^{m+k}(S+1)^{k}\alpha

(5.10)

and

(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\beta=(S-1)^{m}(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\alpha=(S-1)^{m}\gamma.

(5.11)

By (5.1),

\displaystyle\|(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\beta\|_{2}^{2}=\|(S-1)^{m}\gamma\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq p<p^{\prime}\leq m}(-1)^{p+p^{\prime}-1}C_{m}^{p}C_{m}^{p^{\prime}}\|(S^{m-p}-S^{m-p^{\prime}})\gamma\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq p<p^{\prime}\leq m}(-1)^{p+p^{\prime}-1}C_{m}^{p}C_{m}^{p^{\prime}}\|(S^{m-p}-S^{m-p^{\prime}})(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.12)

Thus (5.6) follows from (5.9), (5.10) and (5.12).

Similarly, let
\zeta=(S+1)^{m}\alpha
, then

(S^{2}-1)^{k}\zeta=(S+1)^{m+k}(S-1)^{k}\alpha

(5.13)

and

(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\zeta=(S+1)^{m}(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\alpha=(S+1)^{m}\gamma.

(5.14)

By (5.2),

\displaystyle\|(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\zeta\|_{2}^{2}=\|(S+1)^{m}\gamma\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{m}\sum_{p=0}^{m}C_{m}^{p}\|S^{m-p}\gamma\|^{2}-\sum_{0\leq p<p^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{p}C_{m}^{p^{\prime}}\|(S^{m-p}-S^{m-p^{\prime}})\gamma\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{m}\sum_{p=0}^{m}C_{m}^{p}\|S^{m-p}(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\alpha\|^{2}

\displaystyle-\sum_{0\leq p<p^{\prime}\leq m}C_{m}^{p}C_{m}^{p^{\prime}}\|(S^{m-p}-S^{m-p^{\prime}})(S^{2(k-l)}-S^{2(k-l^{\prime})})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.15)

Therefore, (5.7) holds by (5.13), (5.15) and (5.9) in which
\zeta
in place of
\beta
.
∎

Theorem 5.3
.

\displaystyle\|(S^{m}-S^{n})(S^{p}-S^{q})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}=\|S^{m}(S^{p}-S^{q})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}+\|S^{n}(S^{p}-S^{q})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|(S^{m}-S^{n})S^{p}\alpha\|_{2}^{2}+\|(S^{m}-S^{n})S^{q}\alpha\|_{2}^{2}-\|(S^{m+p}-S^{n+q})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle-\|(S^{m+q}-S^{n+p})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}.

(5.16)

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle[(S^{m}-S^{n})(S^{p}-S^{q})\alpha]_{j}=[(S^{m+p}-S^{m+q}-S^{n+p}+S^{n+q})\alpha]_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{j+m+p}-\alpha_{j+m+q}-\alpha_{j+n+p}+\alpha_{j+n+q},

(5.17)

then (5.16) holds by Proposition 4.16.
∎

More generally, we have

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
P_{k}(\theta)=a_{0}+a_{1}\cos\theta+\cdots+a_{k}\cos k\theta
with
a_{l}\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq l\leq k
,
P_{k}(0)=0
and
P_{k}(\theta)\geq 0
for
\theta\in[0,2\pi)
, then their exist
Q_{k}(z)=b_{0}+b_{1}z+\cdots+b_{k}z^{k}
with
b_{l}\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq l\leq k
, such that

\displaystyle\|Q_{k}(S)\alpha\|_{2}^{2}=\mathrm{bdy}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{k}a_{l}\|(S^{l}-1)\alpha\|_{2}^{2},

(5.18)

for any sequence
\alpha=\{\alpha_{j}\}_{0}^{\infty}\in\ell^{2}
, where

\mathrm{bdy}=\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}|\alpha_{j+l^{\prime}-l}-\alpha_{j}|^{2}-\sum_{j=l}^{l^{\prime}-1}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\right)

(5.19)

in which
\rm{bdy}
is boundary for short and means finite number terms with small indices (cf.
[
2
]
), and
S
is the left shift operator.

Proof.

Let
L_{k}(z)=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=0}^{k}a_{l}(z^{-l}+z^{l})
, then
L_{k}(e^{i\theta})=P_{k}(\theta)\geq 0
for any
\theta\in[0,2\pi]
. That is,
L_{k}(z)
is a Laurent polynomials fulfilling that its restriction on the unit circle is nonnegative. By Fejér-Riesz theorem, there exists a polynomial
Q_{k}(z)=\sum_{l=0}^{k}b_{l}z^{l}
such that

L_{k}(e^{i\theta})=P_{k}(\theta)=|Q_{k}(e^{i\theta})|^{2},\,\,\theta\in[0,2\pi].

(5.20)

Expanding (5.20), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{k}a_{j}(e^{-ij\theta}+e^{ij\theta})=\sum_{l=0}^{k}|b_{l}|^{2}+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}\big(b_{l}\overline{b}_{l^{\prime}}e^{i(l-l^{\prime}\theta)}+\overline{b}_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}e^{-i(l-l^{\prime})\theta}\big)

(5.21)

for any
\theta\in[0,2\pi]
. Comparing coefficients in both sides, we obtain

\begin{cases}a_{0}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}|b_{l}|^{2},\vskip 2.84526pt\\
a_{j}=2\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k,\,l^{\prime}-l=j}\overline{b}_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}=2\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k,\,l^{\prime}-l=j}b_{l}\overline{b}_{l^{\prime}},1\leq j\leq k.\end{cases}

(5.22)

Since
a_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq j\leq k
, by (5.22), we can take
b_{l}\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq l\leq k
. That is,

\begin{cases}a_{0}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}b_{l}^{2},\vskip 2.84526pt\\
a_{j}=2\sum_{0\leq l\leq k-j}b_{l}b_{l+j},1\leq j\leq k,\end{cases}

(5.23)

in which
a_{l},b_{l}\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq l\leq k
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}b_{l}^{2}+2\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{0\leq l\leq k-j}b_{l}b_{l+j}=0

(5.24)

or in another form as follows

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}b_{l}^{2}+2\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}=0

(5.25)

since
P_{k}(0)=0
implying
\sum_{l=0}^{k}a_{l}=0
.

Note that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{l=0}^{k}b_{l}\alpha_{j+l}\Big|^{2}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}b_{l}^{2}|\alpha_{j+l}|^{2}+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\big(\alpha_{j+l}\overline{\alpha}_{j+l^{\prime}}+\overline{\alpha}_{j+l}\alpha_{j+l^{\prime}}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}b_{l}^{2}|\alpha_{j+l}|^{2}+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}(|\alpha_{j+l}|^{2}+|\alpha_{j+l^{\prime}}|^{2})-\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}|\alpha_{j+l}-\alpha_{j+l^{\prime}}|^{2}

(5.26)

then by (5.23), (5.25) and (5.26), for
\alpha=\{\alpha_{j}\}_{0}^{\infty}\in\ell^{2}
,

\displaystyle\|Q_{k}(S)\alpha\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}b_{l}^{2}\|S^{l}\alpha\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}(\|S^{l}\alpha\|_{2}^{2}+\|S^{l^{\prime}}\alpha\|_{2}^{2})

\displaystyle-\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\|(S^{l^{\prime}}-S^{l})\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}b_{l}^{2}\|\alpha\|_{2}^{2}-\sum_{l=0}^{k}b_{l}^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}+2\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\|\alpha\|_{2}^{2}-2\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}

\displaystyle-\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\sum_{j=l}^{l^{\prime}-1}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}-\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\|(S^{l^{\prime}-l}-1)\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}|\alpha_{j+l^{\prime}-l}-\alpha_{j}|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}|\alpha_{j+l^{\prime}-l}-\alpha_{j}|^{2}-\sum_{j=l}^{l^{\prime}-1}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\right)-\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\|(S^{l^{\prime}-l}-1)\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}|\alpha_{j+l^{\prime}-l}-\alpha_{j}|^{2}-\sum_{j=l}^{l^{\prime}-1}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\right)-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{0\leq l\leq k-j}b_{l}b_{l+j}\|(S^{j}-1)\alpha\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq l<l^{\prime}\leq k}b_{l}b_{l^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{l-1}|\alpha_{j+l^{\prime}-l}-\alpha_{j}|^{2}-\sum_{j=l}^{l^{\prime}-1}|\alpha_{j}|^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}a_{j}\|(S^{j}-1)\alpha\|_{2}^{2}.\qed

Remark 5.5
.

As above stated, we have used some special cases of (5.18) to get some concrete sum rules in the last section (for example, in Theorems 4.18, 4.32, 4.51, 4.59 and so on).

Next, we give an explicit expression of
w_{m}
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
. As in Section 4, it is important to know the expression of
w_{m}
for establishing sum rules and higher order Szegő theorems.

In order to do so, we introduce the following notions and notations.

For any formal ordered sum
a_{1}+a_{2}+\cdots+a_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, taking out
j-1
plus signs from its all
n-1
plus signs, the formal sum will be partitioned to
j
different segments. Any result of such operation is called a
j
-partition of the ordered sum
a_{1}+a_{2}+\cdots+a_{n}
and denoted by
\{\mbox{sgm}_{1},\mbox{sgm}_{2},\ldots,\mbox{sgm}_{j}\}
in which
\mbox{sgm}_{l}
is the
l
th segment,
1\leq l\leq j
. If one of such
j
-partitions further satisfies that the numbers of plus signs in its segments are non-increasing from left to right, then this
j
-partition is called a good
j
-partition of the ordered sum
a_{1}+a_{2}+\cdots+a_{n}
. For example,
\{a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3},a_{4},a_{5}\}
,
\{a_{1}+a_{2},a_{3}+a_{4},a_{5}\}
and
\{a_{1},a_{2}+a_{3},a_{4},a_{5}\}
are
3
-partitions of
a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}
. But the former two are good and the latter one is not good. Sometimes we also call
j
-partitions of ordered
n
-tuple
(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n})
since the mapping
a_{1}+a_{2}+\cdots+a_{n}\rightarrow(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{n})
is one to one.

For any formal ordered sum
r_{1}+r_{2}+\cdots+r_{n}
, denote

\displaystyle\alpha^{(r_{1}+r_{2}+\cdots+r_{n})}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{1}}\left(\prod_{\nu=2}^{n-1}\sum_{l_{\nu}=1}^{l_{\nu-1}+r_{\nu}}\right)\alpha_{k+r_{1}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k-1}\prod_{\nu=2}^{n}\alpha_{k+l_{\nu-1}+r_{\nu}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k+l_{\nu-1}-1}.

(5.27)

In what follows,
\mathcal{D}_{m}^{s}=\{\langle r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{s}\rangle:\,\sum_{l=0}^{s}r_{l}=m,r_{l}\in\mathbb{N},1\leq l\leq s\}
, where
\langle r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{s}\rangle
is an unordered
s
-tuple consisting of
s
elements
r_{l}
,
1\leq l\leq s
. That is,
\mathcal{D}_{m}^{s}
is the set of all unordered
s
-decompositions of
m
.

Lemma 5.6
.

For any
n
-tuple
\sigma_{n}^{r}=(r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n})
and any sequence
A=\{A_{j}\}_{0}^{\infty}\in\ell^{1}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{\infty}A_{k_{1}+r_{1}}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{1}+r_{1}}A_{k_{2}+r_{2}}\cdots\sum_{k_{n}=0}^{k_{n-1}+r_{n-1}}A_{k_{n}+r_{n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\xi\in\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}_{n}}^{n-j}}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{\infty}A_{k_{1}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{1}(\sigma^{r}_{n})}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{1}}A_{k_{2}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{2}(\sigma^{r}_{n})}\cdots\sum_{k_{j}=0}^{k_{j-1}}A_{k_{j}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{j}(\sigma^{r}_{n})},

(5.28)

where
\xi=\{\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{1}(\sigma^{r}),\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{2}(\sigma^{r}),\ldots,\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{j}(\sigma^{r})\}
is a
j
-partition of
\sigma^{r}
,
\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}}^{n-j}
is the set of all
j
-partitions of
\sigma^{r}
and

\displaystyle A_{k_{\nu}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{\nu}(\sigma^{r})}=\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{\nu-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{\nu}=2}^{n_{\nu}-n_{\nu-1}-1}\sum_{l_{t_{\nu}}=1}^{l_{t_{\nu}-1}+r_{n_{\nu-1}+t_{\nu}}}A_{k_{\nu}+r_{n_{\nu-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{\nu}=2}^{n_{\nu}-n_{\nu-1}}A_{k_{\nu}+l_{t_{\nu}-1}+r_{n_{\nu-1}+t_{\nu}}}

in which
n_{v}
is the biggest subscript of summands,
r_{\cdot}
, in
\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{\nu}(\sigma^{r})
,
1\leq\nu\leq j
. As a convention,
n_{0}=0
.

Proof.

Take the method of induction.

It is trivial as
n=1
. Assume that (5.28) holds for
n=p
, in the following, we will prove (5.28) also holds as
n=p+1
.

For
\sigma_{p+1}^{r}=(r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{p+1})
and
\sigma_{p}^{r}=(r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{p})
, set

\begin{cases}A^{\prime}_{s+r_{l}}=A_{s+r_{l}},\,\,1\leq l\leq p-1,\vskip 5.69054pt\\
A^{\prime}_{s+r_{p}}=A_{s+r_{p}}\sum_{t=0}^{s+r_{p}}A_{t+r_{p+1}}\end{cases}

(5.29)

for any
s,t\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, then by the assumption of induction,

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{\infty}A_{k_{1}+r_{1}}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{1}+r_{1}}A_{k_{2}+r_{2}}\cdots\sum_{k_{p}=0}^{k_{p-1}+r_{p-1}}A_{k_{p}+r_{p}}\sum_{k_{p+1}=0}^{k_{p}+r_{p}}A_{k_{p+1}+r_{p+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{\infty}A^{\prime}_{k_{1}+r_{1}}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{1}+r_{1}}A^{\prime}_{k_{2}+r_{2}}\cdots\sum_{k_{p}=0}^{k_{p-1}+r_{p-1}}A^{\prime}_{k_{p}+r_{p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}_{p}}^{p-j}}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{\infty}A^{\prime}_{k_{1}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{\prime}}_{1}(\sigma^{r}_{p})}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{1}}A^{\prime}_{k_{2}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{\prime}}_{2}(\sigma^{r}_{p})}\cdots\sum_{k_{j}=0}^{k_{j-1}}A^{\prime}_{k_{j}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{\prime}}_{j}(\sigma^{r}_{p})},

(5.30)

where
\xi^{\prime}=\{\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{\prime}}_{1}(\sigma^{r}),\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{\prime}}_{2}(\sigma^{r}),\ldots,\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{\prime}}_{j}(\sigma^{r})\}
is a
j
-partition of
\sigma^{r}
and

\displaystyle A^{\prime}_{k_{\nu}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{\prime}}_{\nu}(\sigma^{r})}=\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{\nu-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{\nu}=2}^{n_{\nu}-n_{\nu-1}-1}\sum_{l_{t_{\nu}}=1}^{l_{t_{\nu}-1}+r_{n_{\nu-1}+t_{\nu}}}A^{\prime}_{k_{\nu}+r_{n_{\nu-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{\nu}=2}^{n_{\nu}-n_{\nu-1}}A^{\prime}_{k_{\nu}+l_{t_{\nu}-1}+r_{n_{\nu-1}+t_{\nu}}}

(5.31)

in which
n_{v}
is the biggest subscript of summands,
r_{\cdot}
, in
\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{\prime}}_{\nu}(\sigma^{r})
,
1\leq\nu\leq j
.
In particular, since
n_{j}=p
, we have (by (5.29))

\displaystyle A^{\prime}_{k_{j}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{\prime}}_{j}(\sigma^{r})}=\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-1}\sum_{l_{t_{j}}=1}^{l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}A^{\prime}_{k_{j}+r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}}A^{\prime}_{k_{j}+l_{t_{j-1}}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-2}\sum_{l_{t_{j}}=1}^{l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}A_{k_{j}+r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-1}A_{k_{j}+l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}=1}^{l_{p-n_{j-1}-2}+r_{p-1}}A_{k_{j}+l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}+r_{p}}\sum_{k_{p+1}=0}^{k_{j}+l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}+r_{p}}A_{k_{p+1}+r_{p+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-2}\sum_{l_{t_{j}}=1}^{l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}A_{k_{j}+r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-1}A_{k_{j}+l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}=1}^{l_{p-n_{j-1}-2}+r_{p-1}}A_{k_{j}+l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}+r_{p}}\sum_{k_{p+1}=0}^{k_{j}}A_{k_{p+1}+r_{p+1}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{j-1}}+1}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-2}\sum_{l_{t_{j}}=1}^{l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}A_{k_{j}+r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-1}A_{k_{j}+l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}=1}^{l_{p-n_{j-1}-2}+r_{p-1}}A_{k_{j}+l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}+r_{p}}\sum_{k_{p+1}=k_{j}+1}^{k_{j}+l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}+r_{p}}A_{k_{p+1}+r_{p+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{j-1}}+1}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-2}\sum_{l_{t_{j}}=1}^{l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}A_{k_{j}+r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-1}A_{k_{j}+l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}=1}^{l_{p-n_{j-1}-2}+r_{p-1}}A_{k_{j}+l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}+r_{p}}\sum_{k_{j+1}=0}^{k_{j}}A_{k_{j+1}+r_{n_{j}+1}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{j-1}}+1}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-2}\sum_{l_{t_{j}}=1}^{l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}A_{k_{j}+r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-1}A_{k_{j}+l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}=1}^{l_{p-n_{j-1}-2}+r_{p-1}}\sum_{l_{p-n_{j-1}}=1}^{l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}+r_{p}}A_{k_{j}+l_{p-n_{j-1}-1}+r_{p}}A_{k_{j}+l_{p-n_{j-1}}+r_{p+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{j-1}}+1}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}-1}\sum_{l_{t_{j}}=1}^{l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}A_{k_{j}+r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}}A_{k_{j}+l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}\sum_{k_{j+1}=0}^{k_{j}}A_{k_{j+1}+r_{n_{j}+1}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{j-1}}+1}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}}\sum_{l_{t_{j}}=1}^{l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}A_{k_{j}+r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{p-n_{j-1}+1}A_{k_{j}+l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{j-1}}+1}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{n_{j}-n_{j-1}-1}\sum_{l_{t_{j}}=1}^{l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}A_{k_{j}+r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{n_{j}-n_{j-1}}A_{k_{j}+l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}\sum_{k_{j+1}=0}^{k_{j}}A_{k_{j+1}+r_{n_{j}+1}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{j-1}}+1}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{\widetilde{n}_{j}-n_{j-1}-1}\sum_{l_{t_{j}}=1}^{l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}}A_{k_{j}+r_{n_{j-1}+1}}\prod_{t_{j}=2}^{\widetilde{n}_{j}-n_{j-1}}A_{k_{j}+l_{t_{j}-1}+r_{n_{j-1}+t_{j}}},

(5.32)

where
\widetilde{n}_{j}=n_{j}+1=p+1
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the partitions of
\sigma^{r}_{n}=(r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n})
and
\sigma_{n+1}^{r}=(r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{n},r_{n+1})
have the following properties:

(1)

The total number of all partitions of
\sigma^{r}_{n}
is
2^{n-1}
;

(2)

As in (5.32), any one of
j
-partitions of
\sigma^{r}_{n}
corresponds to two partitions of
\sigma^{r}_{n+1}
: one is
j+1
-partition and the other is
j
-partition. (For example, as
n=3
and
j=2
, the
2
-partition,
\{r_{1},r_{2}+r_{3}\}
, of
\sigma^{r}_{3}=(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})
corresponds to the
3
-partition,
\{r_{1},r_{2}+r_{3},r_{4}\}
, and the
2
-partition,
\{r_{1},r_{2}+r_{3}+r_{4}\}
of
\sigma^{r}_{4}=(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})
.

By the above properties, we can get all partitions of
\sigma_{n+1}^{r}
from the ones of
\sigma_{n}^{r}
(just as showing in (5.32)). Thus (5.28) also holds for
n=p+1
by (5.30), (5.32) and the assumption of induction for
n=p
.
∎

For any unordered
n
-tuple
r=\langle r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{s}\rangle
, denote
S_{s}^{r}
be the set of all permutations of the elements (viz.
r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{s}
) of
r
and its element by
\sigma^{r}=(\sigma^{r}_{1},\sigma^{r}_{2},\ldots,\sigma^{r}_{s})
. For instance, if
\sigma^{r}_{l}=r_{l}
,
1\leq l\leq s
, then
\sigma^{r}=(r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{s})
; if
\sigma^{r}_{l}=r_{l+1}
,
1\leq l\leq s-1
and
\sigma^{r}_{s}=r_{1}
, then
\sigma^{r}=(r_{2},r_{3},\ldots,r_{s},r_{1})
.

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{m}^{s}
,
\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}}^{s-j}
,
S^{r}_{s}
and
\xi^{g}
be as above, then for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle w_{m}=\sum_{s=1}^{m}\sum_{r\in\mathcal{D}_{m}^{s}}\sum_{\sigma^{r}\in S_{s}^{r}}\sum_{j=1}^{s-1}\sum_{\xi^{g}\in\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}}^{s-j}}(-1)^{s}c_{s,r}c_{j,\xi^{g}}(j-1)!\prod_{\nu=1}^{j}\odot_{j}\left(\alpha^{\big(\mathrm{sgm}_{\nu}^{\xi^{g}}(\sigma^{r})\big)}\right),

(5.33)

where

\displaystyle c_{s,r}=\frac{1}{t_{1}!t_{2}!\cdots t_{p}!}
with
t_{1}+t_{2}+\cdots+t_{p}=s
in which
t_{l}
,
1\leq l\leq p
, are the multiplicities of
p
different elements in
r
(that is,
r
has
p
different elements,
r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{p}
, whose multiplicities are
t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{p}
respectively);
c_{j,\xi^{g}}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{l_{1}!l_{2}!\cdots l_{q}!},\,\,2\leq j\leq s-1,\vskip 1.42262pt\\
1,\,\,j=1\end{cases}
in which
l_{\nu}
,
1\leq\nu\leq q
, are the multiplicities of different segments of
\xi^{g}
(that is,
\xi^{g}
has
q
different segments,
\xi^{g}_{1}(\sigma^{r}),\xi^{g}_{2}(\sigma^{r}),\ldots,\xi^{g}_{q}(\sigma^{r})
, whose multiplicities are
l_{1},l_{2},\ldots,l_{q}
respectively), and
\alpha^{\big(\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{\nu}(\sigma^{r})\big)}
are given as in (5.27)

Proof.

For
1\leq s\leq m
and
r\in\mathcal{D}_{m}^{s}
, we consider two different cases.

Firstly,
r
is assumed to have distinct elements. That is,
r=\langle r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{s}\rangle
with
r_{k}\neq r_{l}
as
k\neq l
and
r_{1}+r_{2}+\cdots r_{s}=m
.

By Lemma 5.6, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{r}\in S^{r}_{s}}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k_{1}+\sigma^{r}_{1}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{1}-1}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{1}+\sigma^{r}_{1}}\alpha_{k_{2}+\sigma^{r}_{2}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{2}-1}\cdots\sum_{k_{s}=0}^{k_{s-1}+\sigma^{r}_{s-1}}\alpha_{k_{s}+\sigma^{r}_{s}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{s}-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{r}\in S^{r}_{s}}\sum_{j=1}^{s}\sum_{\xi\in\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}}^{s-j}}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k_{1}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{1}(\sigma^{r})}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{1}}\alpha_{k_{2}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{2}(\sigma^{r})}\cdots\sum_{k_{j}=0}^{k_{j-1}}\alpha_{k_{j}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{j}(\sigma^{r})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{r}\in S^{r}_{s}}\sum_{j=1}^{s}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in S_{j}}\sum_{\xi^{g}\in\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}}^{s-j}}\sum_{k_{\tau_{j,1}}=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k_{\tau_{j,1}}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{\tau_{j,1}}(\sigma^{r})}\sum_{k_{\tau_{j,2}}=0}^{k_{\tau_{j,1}}}\alpha_{k_{\tau_{j,2}}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{\tau_{j,2}}(\sigma^{r})}

\displaystyle\cdots\sum_{k_{\tau_{j,j}}=0}^{k_{\tau_{j,j-1}}}\alpha_{k_{\tau_{j,j}}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{\tau_{j,j}}(\sigma^{r})}

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{r}\in S^{r}_{s}}\sum_{j=1}^{s}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in S_{j}}\sum_{\xi^{g}\in\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}}^{s-j}}\sum_{k_{\tau_{j,1}}=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k_{\tau_{j,1}}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{\tau_{j,1}}(\sigma^{r})}\prod_{\nu=2}^{j}\sum_{k_{\tau_{j,\nu}}=0}^{k_{\tau_{j,\nu-1}}}\alpha_{k_{\tau_{j,\nu}}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{\tau_{j,\nu}}(\sigma^{r})},

(5.34)

where
\xi=\{\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{1}(\sigma^{r}),\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{2}(\sigma^{r}),\ldots,\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{j}(\sigma^{r})\}
are
j
-partitions of
\sigma^{r}
,
\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}}^{s-j}
is the set of all
j
-partitions of
\sigma^{r}
,

\displaystyle\alpha_{k_{\nu}+\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{\nu}(\sigma^{r})}=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{r_{n_{\nu-1}}}\prod_{t_{\nu}=2}^{n_{\nu}-n_{\nu-1}}\sum_{l_{t_{\nu}}=1}^{l_{t_{\nu}-1}+r_{n_{\nu-1}+t_{\nu}-1}}\alpha_{k_{\nu}+r_{n_{\nu-1}+1}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{\nu}-1}

\displaystyle\prod_{t_{\nu}=2}^{n_{\nu}-n_{\nu-1}}\alpha_{k_{\nu}+l_{t_{\nu}}+r_{n_{\nu-1}+t_{\nu}}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{\nu}+l_{t_{\nu}}-1}

(5.35)

in which
n_{v}
is the biggest number of summands,
r_{\cdot}
, in
\mathrm{seg}^{\xi}_{\nu}(\sigma^{r})
,
1\leq\nu\leq j
,

\tau_{j}=(\tau_{j,1},\tau_{j,2},\ldots,\tau_{j,j})
,
S_{j}
is the classical symmetric group of order
j
(i.e., the group of all permutations of
\{1,2,\ldots,j\}
) and
\xi^{g}
are good
j
-partitions of
\sigma^{r}
.

The justification for the second equality in (5.34) is that any summand in LHS is a one in RHS vice versa and all summands in each hand side are distinct.

Thus by Theorem 3.11, the sum with single infinite index in (5.34) is

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{r}\in S^{r}_{s}}\sum_{j=1}^{s}\sum_{\xi^{g}\in\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}}^{s-j}}(j-1)!\alpha^{\big(\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{1}(\sigma^{r})\big)}\odot\alpha^{\big(\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{2}(\sigma^{r})\big)}\odot\cdots\odot\alpha^{\big(\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{j}(\sigma^{r})\big)},

(5.36)

where
\alpha^{\big(\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{\nu}(\sigma^{r})\big)}
,
1\leq\nu\leq j
, are given as in (5.27).

Next,
r
is assumed to have
p
distinct elements,
r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{p}
, and their multiplicities are
t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{p}
respectively. Namely,

r=\langle\underbrace{r_{1},r_{1},\cdots,r_{1}}_{t_{1}},\underbrace{r_{2},r_{2},\cdots,r_{2}}_{t_{2}},\cdots,\underbrace{r_{p},r_{p},\cdots,r_{p}}_{t_{p}}\rangle

with
t_{1}r_{1}+t_{2}r_{2}+\cdots+t_{p}r_{p}=m
.
Denote
S^{r}_{s,p}
be the set of all distinct permutations of the elements of
r
. Since their exist the same elements, the number of all distinct permutations in
S^{r}_{s,p}
are
\frac{s!}{t_{1}!t_{2}!\cdots t_{p}!}
. Therefore, repeating any one of the permutations in
S^{r}_{s,p}
for
t_{1}!t_{2}!\cdots t_{p}!
times, we can obtain all permutations of the elements of
r
(with permission of appearing the same permutations) liking the former case. Thus

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{r}\in S^{r}_{s,p}}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k_{1}+\sigma^{r}_{1}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{1}-1}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{1}+\sigma^{r}_{1}}\alpha_{k_{2}+\sigma^{r}_{2}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{2}-1}\cdots\sum_{k_{s}=0}^{k_{s-1}+\sigma^{r}_{s-1}}\alpha_{k_{s}+\sigma^{r}_{s}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{s}-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t_{1}!t_{2}!\cdots t_{p}!}\sum_{\sigma^{r}\in S^{r}_{s}}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k_{1}+\sigma^{r}_{1}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{1}-1}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{1}+\sigma^{r}_{1}}\alpha_{k_{2}+\sigma^{r}_{2}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{2}-1}\cdots\sum_{k_{s}=0}^{k_{s-1}+\sigma^{r}_{s-1}}\alpha_{k_{s}+\sigma^{r}_{s}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{k_{s}-1}.

(5.37)

By Corollary 3.14, the sum with single infinite index in (5.37) is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t_{1}!t_{2}!\cdots t_{p}!}\frac{1}{l_{1}!l_{2}!\cdots l_{q}!}\sum_{\sigma^{r}\in S^{r}_{s}}\sum_{j=1}^{s}\sum_{\xi^{g}\in\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}}^{s-j}}(j-1)!\alpha^{\big(\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{1}(\sigma^{r})\big)}\odot\alpha^{\big(\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{2}(\sigma^{r})\big)}\odot\cdots\odot\alpha^{\big(\mathrm{seg}^{\xi^{g}}_{j}(\sigma^{r})\big)}.

(5.38)

As the statement in Remark 3.10, similar to
w_{l}
,
1\leq l\leq 4
, by Theorem 3.1 and Golinskii-Zlatoš single index theorem (more precisely, Proposition 3.7), (5.33) follows from (3.12), (5.36) and (5.38).
∎

Remark 5.8
.

In fact, by (5.33) and real parts of products of several complex variables (viz. Propositions 4.10, 4.41 and 4.43), we can obtain some general sum rules for
n\in\mathbb{R}
as in the last section for
1\leq n\leq 4
.

Finally, with the above preliminaries, we obtain the following general higher order Szegő theorems.

Theorem 5.9
.

Assume that
\alpha\in\ell^{6}
and
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{3}
, then

\displaystyle Z_{P_{n}}(\mu)=\int_{0}^{2\pi}P_{n}(\theta)\log w(\theta)\frac{d\theta}{2\pi}>-\infty\,\,\Longleftrightarrow\,\,Q_{n}(S)\alpha\in\ell^{2},

(5.39)

where
P_{n},Q_{n}
are given as in Theorem 5.4, and
P_{n}
also satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{a_{0}}{2}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle 2\sum_{l=0}^{\left[\frac{n}{2}\right]}l(l+1)a_{2l+1}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{\left[\frac{n}{2}\right]}(2l-1)(2l+1)a_{2l},\,\,\mbox{as n is odd},\vskip 5.69054pt\\
\displaystyle 2\sum_{l=1}^{\left[\frac{n}{2}\right]}l(l+1)a_{2l-1}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{l=1}^{\left[\frac{n}{2}\right]}(2l-1)(2l+1)a_{2l},\,\,\mbox{as n is even}.\end{cases}

(5.40)

Proof.

Since

\displaystyle P_{n}(\theta)=a_{0}+a_{1}\cos\theta+\cdots+a_{n}\cos n\theta=-\sum_{l=1}^{n}a_{l}(1-\cos l\theta)

(5.41)

in terms of
P_{k}(0)=0
(precisely,
\sum_{l=0}^{n}a_{l}=0
), then

\displaystyle Z_{P_{n}}(\mu)=a_{0}\mathrm{Re}(w_{0})+a_{1}\mathrm{Re}(w_{1})+\cdots+a_{n}\mathrm{Re}(w_{n})=-\sum_{l=1}^{n}a_{l}Z_{n,1}(\mu)

(5.42)

in which
Z_{n,1}(\mu)=\int_{0}^{2\pi}(1-\cos n\theta)\log w(\theta)\frac{d\theta}{2\pi}
.

At first, we consider the series whose general term has only two factors consisting of some elements of
\alpha
and
\overline{\alpha}
in
w_{m}
. For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, by Theorem 5.7, such series in
w_{m}
is the following

-\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{j+m-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j-1}.

(5.43)

By (4.51), its real part is

\displaystyle\mathrm{bdy}-\|\alpha\|^{2}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|(S^{m}-1)\alpha\|^{2}_{2}.

(5.44)

Next, turn to the series which have four factors consisting of some elements of
\alpha
and
\overline{\alpha}
in
w_{m}
. In what follows, we consider them from two different cases.

Case I:
m
is odd. In this case, by Theorem 5.7, such series in
w_{m}
are

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{j+p-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j-1}\alpha_{j+s+q-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j+s-1}\,\,\,\mathrm{and}\,\,\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{j+q-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j-1}\alpha_{j+t+p-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j+t-1}

(5.45)

where
p+q=m
,
1\leq q\leq[\frac{m}{2}]
,
0\leq s\leq p
and
1\leq t\leq q
.

Case II:
m
is even. By Theorem 5.7, such series in this case are

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{j+p-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j-1}\alpha_{j+s+q-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j+s-1}\,\,\,\mathrm{and}\,\,\,\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{j+q-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j-1}\alpha_{j+t+p-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j+t-1},

(5.46)

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{j+\frac{m}{2}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j-1}\alpha_{j+l+\frac{m}{2}-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j+l-1}

(5.47)

and

\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{j+\frac{m}{2}-1}^{2}\overline{\alpha}_{j-1}^{2},

(5.48)

where
p+q=m
,
1\leq q\leq\frac{m}{2}-1
,
0\leq s\leq p
,
1\leq t\leq q
and
1\leq l\leq\frac{m}{2}
.

By (4.51) and Theorem 4.43, we know that the real part of general term,
\alpha_{j+m-l-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j-1}\alpha_{j+k+l-1}\overline{\alpha}_{j+k-1}
, in two cases is a sum whose summands are of three different types as follows

\displaystyle|\alpha_{j+p}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+q}|^{2},\,\,|\alpha_{j+p}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+q}-\alpha_{j+q^{\prime}}|^{2}\,\,\,\mathrm{and}\,\,\,|\alpha_{j+p}-\alpha_{j+p^{\prime}}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+q}-\alpha_{j+q^{\prime}}|^{2}.

(5.49)

By the assumption and using Hölder inequality, the series with general terms given by the latter two types in (5.49) are convergent. By simple calculations, the number of the series with general terms given by the former type in (5.49) is

\begin{cases}[\frac{m}{2}](m+1)=[\frac{m}{2}]\big((p+1)+q\big),\,\,\mbox{if \emph{m} is odd},\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\frac{1}{2}(m^{2}-1)=\frac{m-1}{2}\big((p+1)+q\big)+\frac{m}{2}+\frac{1}{2},\,\,\mbox{if \emph{m} is even}.\end{cases}

(5.50)

Since

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j}|^{4}-\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j+p}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+q}|^{2}=\mathrm{bdy}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big(|\alpha_{j+p}|^{2}-|\alpha_{j+q}|^{2}\big)^{2},

(5.51)

we get that
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j}|^{4}-\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\alpha_{j+p}|^{2}|\alpha_{j+q}|^{2}
is convergent by the assumption.

By (5.40), (5.42), (5.44) and Theorem 5.4, we have

\displaystyle Z_{P_{n}}(\mu)=\mathrm{bdy}+a_{0}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\big[\log(1-|\alpha_{j}|^{2})+|\alpha_{j}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|\alpha_{j}|^{4}\big]-\|Q_{n}(S)\alpha\|_{2}^{2}+R_{n}(\alpha),

(5.52)

where the remainder
R_{n}(\alpha)
is the sum of some series whose general terms are the ones in (5.51), the latter two types in (5.49) and at least six factors consisting of some elements of
\alpha
and
\overline{\alpha}
in
w_{m}
(the last case is for
3\leq m\leq n
).

By the properties of real part of products of several complex variables, it is easy to get that
R_{n}(\alpha)
is finite under the assumption of
\alpha\in\ell^{6}
and
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{3}
. Applying this fact, by Lemma 4.3, (5.39) immediately follows from (5.52) as
\alpha\in\ell^{6}
and
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{3}
.
∎

Theorem 5.10
.

Assume that
\alpha\in\ell^{4}
, then

\displaystyle Z_{P_{n}}(\mu)>-\infty\,\,\Longleftrightarrow\,\,Q_{n}(S)\alpha\in\ell^{2},

(5.53)

where
P_{n},Q_{n}
are given as in Theorem 5.4.

Proof.

It immediately follows from (5.52) under the assumption of
\alpha\in\ell^{4}
.
∎

Remark 5.11
.

This is a general result in
[
7
]
due to Golinskii-Zlatoš.

Theorem 5.12
.

Let
P_{n}(z)=1-\cos n\theta
,
\alpha\in\ell^{6}
and
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{3}
, then

\displaystyle Z_{P_{n}}(\mu)>-\infty\,\,\Longleftrightarrow\,\,\alpha\in\ell^{4}\,\,and\,\,(S^{n}-1)\alpha\in\ell^{2}.

(5.54)

Proof.

Similar to Theorem 5.9.
∎

Proposition 5.13
.

The number of all series in
\alpha^{(r_{1}+r_{2}+\cdots+r_{n})}
is

\mathcal{N}_{r_{1}+r_{2}+\cdots+r_{n}}=\begin{cases}1,\,\,\,n=1,\vskip 5.69054pt\\
r_{1},\,\,\,n=2,\vskip 5.69054pt\\
\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{l=1}^{k}(l+r_{2}),\,\,\,n=3,\vskip 5.69054pt\\
\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{l=1}^{k+r_{2}+r_{3}+\cdots+r_{n-2}}(l+r_{n-1}),\,\,\,n\geq 4.\end{cases}

(5.55)

Proof.

By (5.27), it is trivial for
n=1
and
n=2
.

For
n=3
, by (5.27), the number of all series of
\alpha^{(r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3})}
is

\displaystyle\big(1+2+\cdots+(1+r_{2})\big)+\big(1+2+\cdots+(2+r_{2})\big)+\cdots+\big(1+2+\cdots+(r_{1}+r_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{l=1}^{k}(l+r_{2}).

(5.56)

For
n=4
, noting the following inclusion relation among
l_{\nu}
,
1\leq\nu\leq 3
, given in (5.27),

l_{1}\begin{cases}1\rightarrow l_{2}\begin{cases}1\rightarrow l_{3}\begin{cases}1\\
2\\
\vdots\\
1+r_{3}\end{cases}\\
\vdots\\
1+r_{2}\rightarrow l_{3}\begin{cases}1\\
2\\
\vdots\\
1+r_{2}+r_{3}\end{cases}\end{cases}\\
\vdots\\
r_{1}\rightarrow l_{2}\begin{cases}1\rightarrow l_{3}\begin{cases}1\\
2\\
\vdots\\
1+r_{3}\end{cases}\\
\vdots\\
r_{1}+r_{2}\rightarrow l_{3}\begin{cases}1\\
2\\
\vdots\\
r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}\end{cases}\end{cases}\end{cases}

(5.57)

the number of all series in
\alpha^{(r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}+r_{4})}
is

\displaystyle\big[(1+r_{3})+(2+r_{3})+\cdots+(1+r_{2}+r_{3})\big]+\big[(1+r_{3})+(2+r_{3})+\cdots+(2+r_{2}+r_{3})\big]

\displaystyle+\cdots+\big[(1+r_{3})+(2+r_{3})+\cdots+(r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3})\big]=\sum_{k=1}^{r_{1}}\sum_{l=1}^{k+r_{2}}(l+r_{3}).

(5.58)

For
n\geq 5
, the argument to get (5.55) is similar to (5.58).
∎

Theorem 5.14
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
1\leq s\leq m
, let
\mathcal{C}_{m,s}
be the sum of coefficients of all series which have
2s
factors consisting of
\alpha
and
\overline{\alpha}
in
w_{m}
, then

(1)

\mathcal{C}_{m,1}=1;

(2)

\mathcal{C}_{m,2}=\begin{cases}[\frac{m}{2}](m+1),\,\,\mbox{m is odd},\vskip 2.84526pt\\
\frac{1}{2}(m^{2}-1),\,\,\mbox{m is even};\end{cases}

(3)

For
s\geq 3
,

\mathcal{C}_{m,s}=\sum_{r\in\mathcal{D}_{m}^{s}}\sum_{\sigma^{r}\in S_{s}^{r}}\sum_{j=1}^{s-1}\sum_{\xi^{g}\in\Sigma_{\sigma^{r}}^{s-j}}(-1)^{s}c_{s,r}c_{j,\xi^{g}}(j-1)!\prod_{\nu=1}^{j}\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{sgm}_{\nu}^{\xi^{g}}(\sigma^{r})},

where
\mathcal{N}_{\mathrm{sgm}_{\nu}^{\xi^{g}}(\sigma^{r})}
is given as in (5.55).

Proof.

By Proposition 5.13 and Theorem 5.7.
∎

Theorem 5.15
.

Let
P_{n}(\theta)=\sum_{l=0}^{n}a_{l}\cos l\theta
satisfy

\frac{1}{s}a_{0}=-\sum_{l=s}^{n}a_{l}\mathcal{C}_{l,s}\,\,\,1\leq s\leq n,

(5.59)

where
\mathcal{C}_{l,s}
is given in Theorem 5.14, and assume
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{2}
, then

\displaystyle Z_{P_{n}}(\mu)>-\infty\,\,\Longleftrightarrow\,\,\alpha\in\ell^{2n+2}.

(5.60)

Proof.

Similar to Theorem 5.9.
∎

Proposition 5.16
.

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\cos^{2n-1}\theta=\sum_{l=1}^{n}a_{2n-1,2l-1}\cos(2l-1)\theta

(5.61)

and

\cos^{2n}\theta=\sum_{l=0}^{n}a_{2n,2l}\cos 2l\theta

(5.62)

where the coefficients
a_{k,l}
satisfy the following recursion relations

\displaystyle a_{2n,0}=\frac{1}{2}a_{2n-1,1},\,\,a_{2n,2l}=\frac{1}{2}\big(a_{2n-1,2l-1}+a_{2n-1,2l+1}\big),\,\,a_{2n,2n}=\frac{1}{2}a_{2n-1,2n-1}

(5.63)

in which
1\leq l\leq n-1
, and

\displaystyle a_{2n-1,1}=a_{2(n-1),0}+\frac{1}{2}a_{2(n-1),2},\,\,a_{2n-1,2l-1}=\frac{1}{2}\big(a_{2(n-1),2(l-1)}+a_{2(n-1),2l}\big),

\displaystyle a_{2n-1,2n-1}=\frac{1}{2}a_{2(n-1),2(n-1)}

(5.64)

in which
2\leq l\leq n-1
, as well as
a_{0,0}=1
.

Proof.

By direct calculations and the following trigonometric identities

\cos\theta\cos n\theta=\frac{1}{2}\big[\cos(n-1)\theta+\cos(n+1)\theta\big]

(5.65)

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
∎

Remark 5.17
.

More intuitively, these coefficients can be figured as follows

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccc}&&&&1&&&&\\
&&&\frac{1}{2}&&\frac{1}{2}&&&\\
&&&\frac{3}{4}&&\frac{1}{4}&&&\\
&&\frac{3}{8}&&\frac{1}{2}&&\frac{1}{8}&&\\
&&\frac{5}{8}&&\frac{5}{16}&&\frac{1}{16}&&\\
&\frac{5}{16}&&\frac{15}{32}&&\frac{3}{16}&&\frac{1}{32}&\\
&\frac{35}{64}&&\frac{21}{64}&&\frac{7}{64}&&\frac{1}{64}&\\
\vskip 2.84526pt\vdots&&\vdots&&\vdots&&\vdots&&\vdots\end{array}

In
[
9
]
, Lukic obtained the following

Theorem 5.18

(Lukic)
.

Let
P_{n}(z)=(1-\cos\theta)^{n}
and
(S-1)\alpha\in\ell^{2}
, then

\displaystyle Z_{P_{n}}(\mu)>-\infty\,\,\Longleftrightarrow\,\,\alpha\in\ell^{2n+2}.

(5.66)

When
n=1,2,3,4
, by simple calculations, we can easily find that
P_{n}(\theta)
satisfies (5.59) in all these cases. So the results of Lukic in these cases follow from Theorem 5.15. Based on this observation, we conjecture that
P_{n}(\theta)=(1-\cos\theta)^{n}
is also subject to (5.59) for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
. That is,

Conjecture 5.19
.

Let
P_{n}(z)=(1-\cos\theta)^{n}=\sum_{l=0}^{n}(-1)^{l}C_{n}^{l}\cos^{l}\theta
, then

(1)

As
n=2m
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{s}\sum_{l=0}^{m}C_{2m}^{2l}a_{2l,0}=-\sum_{\nu=s}^{m}\sum_{l=\nu}^{m}C_{2m}^{2l}a_{2l,2\nu}\mathcal{C}_{2\nu,s}+\sum_{\nu=s}^{m}\sum_{l=\nu}^{m}C_{2m}^{2l-1}a_{2l-1,2\nu-1}\mathcal{C}_{2\nu-1,s}

(5.67)

in which
1\leq s\leq 2m
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
;

(2)

As
n=2m-1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{s}\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}C_{2m-1}^{2l}a_{2l,0}=-\sum_{\nu=s}^{m}\sum_{l=\nu}^{m-1}C_{2m-1}^{2l}a_{2l,2\nu}\mathcal{C}_{2\nu,s}+\sum_{\nu=s}^{m}\sum_{l=\nu}^{m}C_{2m-1}^{2l-1}a_{2l-1,2\nu-1}\mathcal{C}_{2\nu-1,s}

(5.68)

in which
1\leq s\leq 2m-1
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
where
\mathcal{C}_{\cdot,\cdot}
and
a_{\cdot,\cdot}
are given as in Theorem 5.14 and Proposition 5.16 respectively.

In addition, we also conjecture the following

Conjecture 5.20
.

P_{n}(\theta)=(1-\cos\theta)^{n}
is the only trigonometric polynomials of order
n
satisfying (5.59).

If such two conjectures are exact, the essence of Lukic result in
[
9
]
rests on (5.59).
